RAG AGENTS — DAILY 118-PROMPT IMPLEMENTATION QUEUE
Repository: https://github.com/Quadron404/RAG-Agents-
Branch: main
Audit time: 2026-11-10 03:07 Asia/Kolkata
Pre-refresh main HEAD: 3a533f6b21e11339a4f992ab1e82545bb262cd51
This file is replaced in full on each daily refresh. It is a living, evidence-based queue, not permission to blindly rerun finished work.

CURRENT VERIFIED BASELINE — 2026-11-10
- The repository is Quadron404/RAG-Agents-, default branch main. The current tree has 298 tracked paths. Re-fetch main before every prompt because another agent may have pushed changes.
- Existing architecture includes a React/Vite frontend, a Cloudflare Worker serving the frontend and owning Google sign-in plus D1 chat/routine state, and a Codespace/FastAPI computer backend with noVNC, CDP browser, terminal, files, and a restricted-browser boundary.
- Existing features found in source include Worker-native Chief chat and SSE, D1 conversations/messages/requests, Google OIDC, separate Gmail consent, connector approvals, owner-only mail sending, recurring routines and Cron, history retrieval, AI Search, incremental and roll-up summaries, task activity, Computer, Files, and settings. Their presence in source is not proof of deployed end-to-end completion.
- The README explicitly states Gmail inbox reading and incoming-reply workflows are not implemented. Existing email sending is owner-bound: the model cannot choose a recipient, and the backend resolves the verified signed-in Google account.
- A first-class channel entity is not established by the current schema. ConversationRow has no channel_id; migration 0003 creates conversations/messages but no channels table. Migration 0004 uses “channel” in the historical sense of a conversation summary. Build a real channel model with safe migrations.
- Chat.tsx currently performs message Edit and Remove in local frontend state, message Pin in localStorage, and conversation Pin/Archive in localStorage. These controls are not server-authoritative and must be made durable or removed.
- AppIcon.tsx contains dock app icons; it does not implement the required dedicated Chief 3×3 glowing-dot face.
- theme.css currently uses violet accents (#7d4bc6 in light mode and #a78bfa in dark mode), not the established RAG deep-burgundy identity #4A0E17.
- The live Worker origin returned HTTP 200. Firecrawl's cloud browser saw only the Google sign-in shell and one Continue with Google link. GET /auth/session returned authenticated:false, user:null. No test-bypass control was visible in the current live session; still audit all source routes and deployment flags rather than relying on old notes.
- Firecrawl mobile inspection reported a 388×842 CSS viewport with no overflow in its isolated mobile frame and a sign-in target about 201×46px. The same run also emitted an earlier 1920px document-width measurement against a 390px body, so treat mobile evidence as partial and retest in AI7 Browserless.
- Authenticated feature journeys were not tested because Firecrawl had no user session. Do not claim they passed.
- The provided Drive avatar reference https://drive.google.com/file/d/15zpTYhbgPD2WFL33eegAS4dXulma3kp/view?usp=sharing currently returns HTTP 404. Preserve the 3×3 glowing-dot requirement, but do not claim the exact reference image was inspected.
- Official Claude Duet material and a hands-on review were researched. Useful patterns include a persistent main conversation, separate project channels, task lists, artifacts, voice, connected apps, background work, proactive briefings, and explicit approval before consequential actions. The review praises the mobile polish and organization but identifies weak follow-through, latency, missing desktop handoff, and limited team sharing. RAG Agents should differentiate with dependable follow-through, responsive desktop/mobile continuity, faster feedback, and optional team collaboration; build an original design rather than copying proprietary UI.
- Reference research: https://claude.com/resources/articles/claude-duet ; https://apps.apple.com/us/app/claude-duet-by-anthropic/id6803276000 ; https://www.chatprd.ai/how-i-ai/claude-duet-review ; https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZIBI6FiLxgQ .
- query.txt was read during this audit and is already empty (0 bytes). Keep the file; do not delete it.

MISSION
Finish RAG Agents as a reliable, production-grade AI workforce. The top priorities are (1) premium, smooth, original Apple-quality UI/UX; (2) correct end-to-end application logic; and (3) exact agreement between UI state and backend reality. Chief is the single visible assistant. Specialists remain internal. The main conversation and focused channels must feel like one unified workspace. Prioritize mobile and desktop equally.

Frontend/src/{App.tsx,auth.ts,core.ts,store.ts,views/Chat.tsx,views/Tasks.tsx,views/Computer.tsx,views/Files.tsx,views/Settings.tsx,views/Bots.tsx,components/Shell.tsx,components/AppIcon.tsx,components/ApprovalCard.tsx,components/RoutinesPanel.tsx,styles/theme.css}
worker/src/{index.ts,store.ts,connector-approvals.ts,routines.ts,scheduler.ts,chat/routes.ts,chat/store.ts,chat/types.ts,chat/history.ts,chat/search.ts,chat/summaries.ts,chat/routine-tools.ts,chat/mail-tool.ts,chat/connector-tools.ts}
worker/migrations/*.sql, worker/test/*.spec.ts, backend/app/{main.py,auth/,computer/,restricted/,workspace/,email/}, backend/tests/, codespace/.

MANDATORY EXECUTION CONTRACT — REPEAT THIS FOR EVERY PROMPT
1. Re-fetch current main, read AI7_PROGRESS.md and query.txt, inspect the exact source, tests, D1 schema, and deployed behavior relevant to the prompt. Never trust this header as a substitute for a fresh audit.
2. Classify the task COMPLETE, PARTIAL, BROKEN, or MISSING. If COMPLETE and passing with current evidence, do not repeat it; record the evidence and move to the next genuinely missing item. If PARTIAL or BROKEN, repair only the real gap. Do not create cosmetic churn to show activity.
3. Implement a complete vertical slice: responsive UI, accessible controls, state transitions, validation, server-side authorization, persistence, error recovery, tests, and user-visible proof. No mock success, dead buttons, frontend-only persistence, fake task progress, or invented API routes.
4. Preserve the React + Cloudflare Worker + D1 + Codespace architecture unless a measured, documented reason justifies a change. Use additive, reversible migrations; preserve existing records, IDs, summary coverage, and user data. Do not weaken authentication, CSRF, approval, restricted-browser, or owner-isolation boundaries.
5. Use the user's original RAG identity: deep burgundy #4A0E17 as a restrained signature, excellent typography and spacing, purposeful motion, refined surfaces, and genuinely responsive desktop/tablet/mobile behavior. Do not produce a generic AI dashboard or clone Claude's proprietary visuals.
6. Add focused unit, integration, API-contract, migration, security, and frontend tests. Check each package's actual scripts before running commands. Run the relevant suites plus frontend build/typecheck, Worker tests/typecheck, and backend tests where applicable. Report exact commands and results.
7. For browser-facing implementation acceptance, use the configured AI7 remote Browserless MCP when available: Import → browserless → remote → https://mcp.browserless.io/mcp. Do not substitute local Chrome, local Playwright, Firecrawl, or RAG Agents' own Computer. If AI7 Browserless is unavailable, mark the browser check BLOCKED with evidence; do not claim a pass.
8. After a prompt changes application code and all relevant tests pass, deploy the updated frontend assets and Cloudflare Worker to the existing production deployment. Use the already-configured CLOUDFLARE_API_KEY and GITHUB_TOKEN environment secrets. Never print, echo, log, commit, paste, or expose credentials. Verify Wrangler's actual authentication behavior; do not invent a token variable or ask the owner for credentials.
9. Verify the deployed origin and the real affected user journey. Record commit SHA, Worker version ID, timestamp, tests, browser run ID/screenshots where available, and unresolved issues. Never claim deployment or feature success without evidence. Keep the last-known-good deployment and data safe.
10. For every failure, use the compulsory loop: reproduce → capture evidence → trace root cause → make a durable fix → add/run regression tests → deploy → retest the same scenario against the deployed version. Repeat until it passes. If an external prerequisite is genuinely unavailable, record BLOCKED precisely and continue independent work.
11. Append progress to AI7_PROGRESS.md after each prompt. Keep a durable ledger with prompt number, outcome, files changed, migration, tests, deployment version, Browserless evidence, and remaining blockers. Do not erase prior progress.
12. If you need owner clarification, a missing external permission, or an unavailable credential, write a concise, non-secret entry to query.txt with exact context, attempted resolution, and the decision required. Continue unrelated tasks. Do not clear query.txt; the Chief of Staff reads and clears it in the next daily refresh.
13. Never send real external email, delete user data, publish files, or perform irreversible external actions during tests. Use a designated test identity and synthetic data; require the product's explicit approval flow for consequential actions.
14. Do not modify prompt.txt or prompt2.txt while executing this queue. Do not create new schedules or ask the owner to hand off to another mode. Work autonomously within the available environment and report truthful blockers.
15. Execute the queue in order, but skip only tasks proven complete by current evidence. If all 118 are complete, create a new evidence-based backlog instead of recycling completed prompts.

DAILY ALLOCATION
Reserve early prompts for real defects and security gaps. If no release-blocking defect exists, convert that capacity into new verified product capability. Use the remaining prompts for new, non-duplicate goals. The goal is a finished product, not endless redesign.

Prompt 1: Production authentication boundary and test-bypass audit

OBJECTIVE AND IMPLEMENTATION
Audit every public Worker route, backend route, frontend entry point, environment flag, and test helper for any sign-in bypass, impersonation path, shared test identity, or developer-only shortcut. The current live Firecrawl session showed only Google sign-in and /auth/session returned authenticated:false; do not repeat an obsolete claim that a bypass is visibly present. Verify whether any hidden route or deployment flag can still mint a session or expose /threads, /file, /computer, /websockify, /ai, or user data without a valid session. Remove or hard-disable any production bypass while keeping local test fixtures isolated. Add negative tests for anonymous HTTP and WebSocket access and prove authenticated developer mode cannot grant identity or ownership. Never add a public bypass to make QA easier.

REQUIRED ACCEPTANCE
- Verify the user-visible behavior from the actual application, not only from source code.
- Verify persistence, owner isolation, loading/empty/error states, refresh behavior, and cross-device behavior wherever relevant.
- Add regression tests for the exact failure modes and run the relevant build and test suites.
- Deploy the changed frontend assets and Worker only after checks pass, then verify the deployed journey and record the evidence in AI7_PROGRESS.md.

Prompt 2: Session-cookie and token lifecycle hardening

OBJECTIVE AND IMPLEMENTATION
Trace the complete Google OIDC and session lifecycle through Worker code, D1 migrations, cookies, and frontend bootstrap. Verify PKCE, state, nonce, issuer, audience, email verification, session hashing, rotation, expiry, Secure, HttpOnly, SameSite, path, and cache behavior. Add tests for replayed state, expired sessions, cookie tampering, rotation, clock skew, and parallel login. Fix the smallest root cause and ensure the frontend never stores bearer credentials in localStorage or exposes them to agents, logs, URLs, or browser-visible error text.

OBJECTIVE AND IMPLEMENTATION
Make legitimate sessions recover cleanly after reload, browser restart, device switch, network interruption, and expired-cookie responses. Confirm /auth/session is authoritative and that a stale frontend state cannot keep showing a signed-in workspace after the server rejects the session. Add a clear re-authentication recovery screen that preserves only safe, non-sensitive drafts and returns the user to the intended channel after login. Test two independent browser contexts for one account and ensure logging out one session follows the documented revocation policy without corrupting other sessions.

Prompt 5: Complete multi-tenant data-isolation audit

OBJECTIVE AND IMPLEMENTATION
Build a route-by-route ownership matrix for users, conversations, messages, summaries, AI Search documents, routines, approvals, files, computer sessions, and audit records. Attempt cross-owner reads, writes, deletes, ID substitution, guessed identifiers, and replayed approval IDs in tests. Every query must bind ownership from the authenticated principal, not model arguments or request bodies. Add regression tests for D1 and backend paths, including WebSocket upgrades and internal gateway headers. Do not loosen the existing internal-token boundary.

REQUIRED ACCEPTANCE
- Verify the user-visible behavior from the actual application, not only from source code.

Prompt 45: Approval lifecycle, expiry, and replay protection

OBJECTIVE AND IMPLEMENTATION
Harden approval records against duplicate decisions, expired cards, owner mismatch, replay, and concurrent tabs. Ensure approval is bound to an exact immutable action payload and cannot be repurposed after the user approves it. Add idempotency keys, expiration tests, denial tests, and recovery when the downstream provider fails after approval. The UI must never claim an action happened merely because approval was granted.

Audit the Files view and daemon bridge for listing, reading, writing, upload, download, and path handling. Enforce canonical path containment and owner/session checks; reject traversal and symlink escapes. Preserve binary files and verify checksums or sizes after transfer. Test large files, interrupted downloads, Windows-style paths, and unavailable Codespace state.
